  - because: Backing the home side of the first-five-innings moneyline because 1 pre-registered signal fired: (1) this lineup's top-of-order minus bottom-of-order strength (top_minus_bottom; each side's number describes the matchup that side's own lineup faces): away 0.015 wOBA, home 0.045 wOBA (this game's own values rest on: away over 5,938 plate-appearance denominators; home over 8,795 plate-appearance denominators); a gap of 0.030 wOBA, clearing the threshold of 0.023 wOBA (rung 1 of 3, the 50th percentile of that gap over the ladder's derivation sample, 4,838 games with both sides measured, 2023-24). This mechanism's direction was frozen before any search ran at +1 (the side holding more of it is the side the mechanism favours), so the signal points to the home side. Pre-registered reason it should matter: a top-heavy order concentrates its best bats where the extra plate appearances go, and club-level pricing averages that concentration away; the weakest mechanism of the six, kept because V4 stated it before seeing its result. This is a signal count, not a forecast: it publishes no probability of its own, says nothing about whether the market's price is mistaken, and attaches no claim of value -- none has been earned.   - because: This pick carries the market's own probability and no edge, by construction. Its p_model is the board's own de-vigged consensus for this exact selection, republished unchanged by analyze()'s PROJECT phase under the identity map -- zero fitted parameters, no recalibration intercept, no Platt, no isotonic refit (docs/PREREG_CALIBRATED_PROBABILITY.md §2). It is published as a calibration reference -- how good the market's own number is -- never as a reason to bet.
